I would like to inform Mr Maier and those who may have followed him on the same subject: There were always legal remedies against fees, but these did not have any suspensive effect. Now the appeals have a suspensive effect, but they should not be brought in on a voluntary basis, for example only to obtain payment support. This would be inadmissible and certainly not a rule of law. Anyone who abuses the right to appeal must expect a penitentiary sentence. So here, too, a step has been taken towards justice and equality and no harm or difficulty has been done to anyone. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-10-24-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00081_d7e13021	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! It has already been pointed out that we are now implementing a European directive that facilitates the in-transport transmission of radio and telecommunications facilities. There is much to be said about this. I regret that, in particular, by the Greens, by Dr. Moser, things have been said here that in a number of areas contribute to the actual health damage to the population. [[Abg. Dr. Moser: I just wanted to address the treatment of the mobile petition, nothing else!]] It is true that there are a number of medical studies that have been carried out in the area of noise and prove that, apart from an objective burden on the population with whatever impunity, there are also subjective nuisances that actually burden the population. That is, the feeling that something is bothering me is much more straining to me than the actual, objective burden I have to bear. What does this mean for the radio system discussion? . We know that the ÖNORM and the European limit values do not, in fact, expect, as always, any health damage or impairment of people. There is no medical evidence that such a thing can happen with the limit values we hold. However, it is well justified by the population to question whether these Ö-NORMS and limit values are actually up to date in science. We know that these limits were sufficiently confirmed at the last major conferences, as by far enough, to a thousand times. However, when a person sees a new radio antenna in front of his house and does not know that his health cannot be affected by it, he has an irrational fear. These fears are constantly being picked up, affecting people by far the most. That is why I am pleased that the municipality has now concluded an agreement with all mobile operators, which ensures that the population is informed. In other words, all mobile operators have committed themselves to providing information on the individual radio systems to the municipalities and to providing them with contact addresses, because intensive exchanges with the population are certainly the best way of ensuring the safety of the health sector by far. I believe that this has taken sufficient account of the concerns of the population and that it is not necessary to provide for a further participation of neighbouring people in construction regulations or similar arrangements. Everyone interested in how the radio system is designed now has access to the discussion and information. It was important to ensure this. To the other discussion that has been attracted today, namely that the Telekom operators – in particular Deutsche Telekom – are now subject to asymmetrical competition, I have to say as a New Economy speaker: The most important thing that must be at the heart of all of us here is that the innovative power of our companies is preserved. In other words, it must also be certain that those companies that make innovative and highly resourced investments in infrastructure will have a certain degree of certainty that their investments are at least cost-effective. I therefore believe that we need to have a discussion on this. The fact that the market is now working in this area is due to the excellent conditions of competition, which are already in place, thanks to the privatisation of Deutsche Telekom, which are quite successful in European comparison. In that, in a number of areas where this market is now functioning, which is not yet the case, we have to re-examine and review individual regulations, I believe we agree in all political groups. To sum up, it must be said that the effort must be made to create a high-performance broadband network in the Internet, the most favourable voice telephony and the possibility of innovation, especially in the broadband network, particularly in the UMTS area and the new communications media, so that we will be at the top of the European states in the future. This is particularly relevant to the question of the economic location. In the future, the establishment and opportunities of new, innovative companies in a knowledge society will be the basis for our social security and our economic existence. In this sense, I hope that all of us will continue to work together. I am firmly convinced that we are on a very, very good path. Thank you.

I assume that this petition will come to everyone's agreement, for the reason alone, because the implementation does not cause any costs. What does this petition mean in detail? . It is about changing the road transport rules to include optically distorted zebra strips where it makes sense, where experts find that it is a good way to improve road safety. What are optically distorted zebra strips? – It is only a special form of the application of surfaces that give the approaching driver the impression that there is a floor threshold. This means that this supposed floor swelling clearly triggers a reaction in the driver, which reduces speed, with a lower speed clearly meaning greater safety for pedestrians. [[Intrusion of the Abg. ParlaMint-AT_2001-10-24-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00081_d7e16388	Thank you very much, Mr President. Thank you very much, Mr State Secretary! High house! My remarks concern the agreement between the Republic of Austria and the Federal Republic of Germany to avoid double taxation in the field of income tax and wealth. Tax relations between these two countries are currently under the double taxation agreement of 4. October 1954, as amended by the Agreement of 8 October 1954. In July 1992 the Commission adopted a regulation on the application of the principle of equal treatment for men and women. At Germany's request, negotiations on an overall revision were started in January 1996, which could be concluded in June 2001. Our representatives in the Ministry of Finance managed to avoid the location regulations that were desired by Germany but which were discriminatory for Austria. The new DBA mainly follows the OECD pattern agreement on income and wealth taxes. Dear ladies and gentlemen! Allow me to deal briefly with a few points. The new definition of establishment follows the OECD concept; the catalogue of exceptions has been extended. New guidelines are also in force in the treatment of artistic and sports incomes. In future, commercial musicians as well as artistic musicians will be subject to tax in the state of activity. However, a certain income is applied by Germany in all accounting methods. Tax exemption is granted when art and sports events are mainly promoted from public funds of the State of dispatch. The new agreement constitutes a balanced compromise, with Germany initially pursuing targets that would have been detrimental to the Austrian site. Austria's insistence on the old state of agreement would have been totally unrealistic in view of Germany's legal objectives. Nor should it be overlooked that the modernisation of the Treaty also seems to be quite beneficial from the point of view of Austrian economic interests. Dear ladies and gentlemen! I would ask you to give your consent to this double taxation agreement.
